# Data.Machine

Fixed-width and target-sized scalar operations. Names make checked, wrapping, or saturating overflow policy explicit.

<div class="doc-mod-bar"><div class="doc-mod-bar-primary"><span class="support-tag support-tag--current">Embedded</span> <code>import Data.Machine</code></div><div class="doc-mod-bar-links"><a href="/library/source/Data.Machine/">View source</a><a href="/library/search/?q=%2BData.Machine">Search module</a><a href="/reference-sources/Data.Machine.cinch" download="Data.Machine.cinch">Download .cinch</a><a href="/library/00-index/">All modules</a></div></div>

**Dependencies:** [Algebra.Arithmetic](Algebra.Arithmetic.md), [Data.Option](Data.Option.md), [Data.Ordering](Data.Ordering.md), [Text.Codec](Text.Codec.md).

<details class="doc-notes"><summary>Module notes</summary>

Data.Machine: the machine scalar types and their operations.

The machine scalar types follow the primitive types specification:
`I8`-`I64`, `U8`-`U64`, `Usize`, `Isize`, `F32` and `F64` are core
runtime types. Names follow decision D6: an operation is a prelude
name of the form `<op><Type>` (`wrapping-add-u32`) and a conversion
is `<src>To<dst>` (`u8-to-i32`). The `checked*`, `saturating*` and
`wrapping*` conversion families capitalize the source type
(`checked-u8-to-i64`); the total family lowercases it (`u8-to-i32`).
The specification's dotted `U32.wrappingAdd` spelling is a
presentation form (the presentation aliases in the checker).

Overflow behavior is never implicit: `checked` returns Option and
never the wrapped value, `wrapping` is two's complement wrap,
`saturating` clamps to the bounds. There is no Add/Sub/Mul/Neg
instance for machine types: a class method has a single result type
and cannot express which family is meant (primitive types
specification section 8). `F32` and `F64` have no Eq/Ord instances.

Module scheme (design/base-library.md): this is the machine
scalars' vocabulary home (the scheme names the mathematical types;
the machine family groups here), carrying its instance family
(Eq/Ord per integer type, `Bits` over the integers, and the `I64`
`Text.Codec` instance that matches the A6 codec generator's INTEGER
column: 8 bytes little-endian).

</details>

</article>

<article class="doc-entry" id="v-f64-to-decimal">
<div class="doc-sig-bar"><code class="doc-sig"><strong class="doc-decl-name tok-variable">f64-to-decimal</strong> :: <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/library/Data.Machine/" title="F64">F64</a> <span class="tok-operator">-&gt;</span> <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/library/Data.Bytes/" title="Bytes">Bytes</a></code><span class="doc-sig-links"><a href="#v-f64-to-decimal" title="Permalink">#</a><a href="/library/source/Data.Machine/#L1219" title="View source at line 1219">Source</a></span></div>
<div class="doc-body">

The P31 text conversions of `F64` (seed/runtime/src/text.rs;
the `cinch.runtime.textF64*` intrinsics, nm-verified there):
one float's shortest round-trip decimal text (scientific form)
and the float one decimal spelling stands for (correctly
rounded). A spelling that is not a decimal number takes the
defined `MalformedDecimal` fault (exit 71).

</div>
</article>

<article class="doc-entry" id="v-i8-to-i8">
<div class="doc-sig-bar"><code class="doc-sig"><strong class="doc-decl-name tok-variable">i8-to-i8</strong> :: <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/library/Data.Machine/" title="I8">I8</a> <span class="tok-operator">-&gt;</span> <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/library/Data.Machine/" title="I8">I8</a></code><span class="doc-sig-links"><a href="#v-i8-to-i8" title="Permalink">#</a><a href="/library/source/Data.Machine/#L504" title="View source at line 504">Source</a></span></div>
<div class="doc-body">

Primitive types specification, section 7: explicit conversions for
every ordered pair of numeric machine types. The total family
(`u8-to-i32`) exists when the conversion preserves every value, and
for integer-to-float and float-to-float, which round per IEEE 754.
`checked` returns Option (None on NaN and out-of-range floats),
`saturating` clamps (NaN maps to zero), `wrapping` truncates in
two's complement and is defined between integer types only. A
total integer conversion's result is its source's value: it states
that (`r == x`, a fact over the argument wherever the argument has
a reading) and the source's range (`InRange 0 255 r` for a `U8`,
which holds even where it has none), so a converted byte is a
non-negative `I64` for `nat-from-i64` and below 256 wherever that
matters.

</div>
</article>

<article class="doc-entry" id="v-mask-i64">
<div class="doc-sig-bar"><code class="doc-sig"><strong class="doc-decl-name tok-variable">mask-i64</strong> :: <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/library/Data.Machine/" title="I64">I64</a> <span class="tok-operator">-&gt;</span> (<span class="tok-variable">m</span> :: <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/library/Algebra.Arithmetic/#t-InRange" title="Algebra.Arithmetic.InRange">InRange</a> 0 9223372036854775807 <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/library/Data.Machine/" title="I64">I64</a>) <span class="tok-operator">-&gt;</span> <span class="tok-variable">refined</span> (0 &lt;= <span class="tok-variable">cinch-result</span> &amp;&amp; <span class="tok-variable">cinch-result</span> &lt;= <span class="tok-variable">m</span>) <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/library/Data.Machine/" title="I64">I64</a></code><span class="doc-sig-links"><a href="#v-mask-i64" title="Permalink">#</a><a href="/library/source/Data.Machine/#L1194" title="View source at line 1194">Source</a></span></div>
<div class="doc-body">

A mask with a non-negative mask word: the `bit-and` of any word
with `m >= 0` lies in `[0, m]` (the and clears the sign bit and
every bit `m` lacks), so `mask-i64 x 255` is a byte. It is the same
intrinsic as `bit-and-i64`; only the declared range differs, which
`bit-and-i64` itself cannot state because a negative mask (`-1`,
the high bit) is an ordinary use of it.

</div>
</article>

<div class="doc-sig-bar"><code class="doc-sig"><strong class="doc-decl-name tok-variable">u8-to-nat</strong> :: <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/library/Data.Machine/" title="U8">U8</a> <span class="tok-operator">-&gt;</span> <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/library/Algebra.Arithmetic/#t-InRange" title="Algebra.Arithmetic.InRange">InRange</a> 0 255 <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/library/Data.Nat/" title="Nat">Nat</a></code><span class="doc-sig-links"><a href="#v-u8-to-nat" title="Permalink">#</a><a href="/library/source/Data.Machine/#L1199" title="View source at line 1199">Source</a></span></div>
<div class="doc-body">

The unsigned machine integers as the `Nat` of the same value: the
widening conversion's range is the evidence `nat-from-i64` needs,
and the result keeps it.

</div>
</article>
